oalcheg and candidate cores · star-label comparison

The opening is not the sole source of atypical spelling. Shorter cores remain candidates, but their rankings depend on the segmentation, transcription and reference labels.

Rules were frozen before the transformed scores. The original word and prior raw results were already exposed. All rankings are descriptive percentiles among exact-length / exact-training-frequency peer types, not p-values, confidence levels or evidence of phonetic writing.

1 · Zodiac figure/star labels

Train on the six other surviving sign identities assigned local hand4; exclude both Aries and both Taurus panels. H and V trained separately, as in v82. H209 / V194 original readable training tokens. Every eligible training and comparison token receives the same transformation. Literal removal changes only words beginning with that exact opening.

Cells show percentile (number of other matched types), minimum five peers. A100 means above all available peers, sometimes only a handful.

RuleCandidateH charactersH groupsV charactersV groups
No removaloalcheg92.9 (14)100.0 (19)92.9 (14)100.0 (17)
Remove literal o-alcheg94.4 (18)100.0 (23)88.9 (18)100.0 (22)
Remove literal oa-lcheg100.0 (8)100.0 (9)100.0 (10)100.0 (8)
Remove literal oal-cheg90.0 (10)87.5 (8)57.1 (7)100.0 (5)

alcheg stays high, although V character rank is88.9 rather than the old90 display cutoff. lcheg ranks above every matched peer in all four views. cheg is unstable: H87.5–90.0, V57.1–100.0, with only5–10 peers. Those differences do not select a root.

2 · Equal-width sensitivity

As a broader alternative, remove the first1/2/3 units from every label, regardless of the initial symbols. Units are EVA characters or fixed longest ch/sh/cth/ckh/cph/cfh groups. This avoids giving only the candidate permission to lose its opening, but assumes a uniform prefix width that is not established. Empty residues are excluded with counts preserved.

RuleCandidateH charactersH groupsV charactersV groups
Remove first unit everywherealcheg92.9 (14)100.0 (19)92.9 (14)100.0 (17)
Remove first2 units everywherelcheg100.0 (13)100.0 (14)100.0 (12)100.0 (11)
Remove first3 units everywherecheg100.0 (12)100.0 (10)91.7 (12)100.0 (10)

These sensitivities retain high ranks too. Peer sets and learned distributions change with removal, so percentile differences are not a calibrated measurement of a prefix effect. All transformed-string collisions are preserved in MODELS.json; collapsed forms are counted as one peer type.

3 · What remains unusual?

Under the H/V zodiac character models, o itself is a common beginning. The full word contains a rare o→a transition and unseen l→c and e→g transitions. Grouping ch changes l→c into l→ch; it does not turn the written pattern into phonemes. Removing o or oa leaves the l/ch and e/g junctions. Removing oal leaves e→g as an unseen transition in both readings. Thus the remaining unusualness is not simply proof of an unusual proper-name root.

These are additive model-score components, not causal percentages of linguistic unusualness. The written g occurs3 times in H zodiac training and4 times in V; all proposed cores keep it.

4 · Detached astronomical star labels

Compare separately with f68r1/f68r2 S labels:53 physical loci, H/U/V kept separate. Train one page and rank against transformed types on the other; repeat the reverse direction. These small models contain only20–30 readable training tokens. No g occurs on either reference page in H/U/V; every candidate therefore includes an unseen symbol mapped to the explicit unknown bucket. Core rankings here are sparse, model-sensitive diagnostics, not matched replication of zodiac findings. f68r3 cluster/connector labels are excluded from this individual-star cohort.

5 · Alternate readings and interpretation

H/K/V/U all oalcheg; this agreement concerns one source location, not independent sample replication. F absent. U/K do not have comparable six-sign coverage for the zodiac baseline; their original target rows stay visible.

Linked non-star body-text forms remain H okalcheg versus C okal.cheg and F/U okalchem on f39r, and H lcheg versus F lchem on f103r. These do not replace the four agreeing readings of the Aries anchor.

Result: functional openings remain a viable hypothesis. Their removal does not generally erase the measured anomaly, but the data do not identify a content core or establish phonetic status. The next useful check is the g-ending and its neighbouring signs across the agreed label and disputed text forms, retaining each image reading. Historical names have not been fitted.

6 · Complete source readings

H/Takahashi, U/Stolfi, V/Grove, K/Kluge-from-Petersen, and other available source codes are not independent samples. Null barriers and raw comments remain visible.
